On the predicate: the W0 log names three that drifted once copied (`spacing_step`, `approved_onboarded_users`, `CHUNK_PRESENTED_AND_DUE_SQL`), and S18d's row says the view exists because "six hand-rolled JOINs drift; a missed site silently keeps delivering to a revoked user". The module deciding who gets a session is the worst possible place for a seventh copy. **Checked rather than assumed: #60's `apps.bot` imports are function-local inside `delivery_lister_ids()` (`access_control.py:273`), so calling `is_approved` pulls no Telegram into `apps/api`** — #60 therefore stays exactly what it was and is not a defect forcing duplication. An AST test fails any file this slice added that grows its own `FROM access_requests`. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: revocation takes effect on the learner's next request, because `resolve_session` re-checks approval per request — not by invalidating sessions inside `revoke_access`.** | The alternative is cheaper per request but requires editing an existing file under `packages/core/services/`, which this slice's out-of-scope rule forbids, and it would still leave live sessions for any future path that revokes. Cost of the chosen answer: one index probe on a three-row table per request. `get_current_user` still calls exactly one service function, because `resolve_session` composes `is_approved` itself — CLAUDE.md §2's "one service function" is about the *route*, and the auth dependency is that sentence's *authorises* step. A mid-session revocation test is the only way to observe the behaviour it buys. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: RP ID is `foundgrant.com`, not `app.foundgrant.com`; expected origin on assertion is `https://app.foundgrant.com`.** `core.config` refuses to start when the RP ID is not a registrable-domain suffix of the origin's host. | A browser scopes a credential to its RP ID permanently. Scoping to the subdomain means a second subdomain can never use it, and changing it later costs **every learner a full re-enrolment**. The startup check exists because a mismatch is a browser-side `SecurityError` thrown before any request is sent — the server sees nothing at all, and the phone just says something went wrong. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: the session cookie is host-only on `api.foundgrant.com` — `__Host-ee_session`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `Path=/`, 30-day sliding.** | `app.` and `api.` are different **origins** but the same **site**, and SameSite is decided by site, so `Lax` is sent on the cross-origin fetch and `None` would only additionally expose the cookie to unrelated sites. No `Domain` means the Vercel edge never receives the session. Sliding rather than absolute because the criterion reads as 30 days of *inactivity*: an absolute window would sign a daily user out on day 31. **Accepted consequence:** Next middleware and server components cannot gate routes, so the frontend guard is client-side and the API is the enforcement point (new issue #74). |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: CSRF is held by the CORS preflight and the origin allow-list, not by `SameSite=Lax`.** Every state-changing route is a JSON `POST`; **no auth route may ever accept a form encoding**, and `allow_headers` must keep requiring `Content-Type`. | `SameSite` is decided by *site*, so a hostile or compromised subdomain of `foundgrant.com` would be same-site and carry the cookie — `Lax` only excludes unrelated domains. A JSON `POST` is never a simple request, so it always preflights, and the preflight is answered only for the two allowed origins. Adequate at two learners with no other subdomains, and it **becomes load-bearing at W3** when `POST /correct` arrives. Written down now so W3 inherits a stated position and the next person reaching for `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` knows what it would remove. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: rate limits are two Postgres-backed buckets per request — a salted hash of the client address, and a global ceiling — enforced in one `apps/api/deps.py` dependency; and `english-api.service` gains `--proxy-headers --forwarded-allow-ips 127.0.0.1`.** | Redis is banned and in-process counters do not survive `--workers 2`. The hash keeps a raw address out of the record (CLAUDE.md §5). Per-client alone is evaded from many addresses; global alone lets one attacker deny sign-in to the two learners. Without `--proxy-headers` every request behind Caddy reports `127.0.0.1` and the per-client half collapses into one bucket — **a failure that looks exactly like success** (new issue #76). |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: sessions and claim tokens are stored as plain SHA-256 digests, not Argon2/bcrypt.** | Both are 256-bit CSPRNG values, not human-chosen passwords: there is no dictionary to slow down. A KDF would add latency to every authenticated request and defend against nothing the digest does not already cover. Lookup is by primary key on the digest, constant-time by construction. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: a sign-count regression is a hard 401 and the credential is *not* disabled; `stored == 0 and new == 0` is not a regression.** | Apple passkeys always report 0, so treating 0-then-0 as a regression would lock both learners out on day one — `py_webauthn` enforces exactly this rule at `verify_authentication_response.py:159`. On a genuine regression the legitimate authenticator's counter keeps rising and keeps working while the clone's stays below and keeps failing; disabling the credential would lock out the victim alongside the clone. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: the WebAuthn challenge is consumed in its own committed transaction, before verification — while the claim token is consumed only on success.** | Found while implementing: sharing one transaction meant a failed signature check rolled the consumption back and left the challenge live for the rest of its five minutes, giving an attacker unlimited tries against one nonce. A challenge is spent by being *answered*, not by being answered correctly. The claim token is the opposite, so the two are deliberately in different transactions. A test asserts a bad attempt burns the challenge. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: migration 009 carries the three `users` columns + the view recreate + five `auth_*` tables, and `docs/TASKS-v3-web.md`'s numbering table was edited in the same commit to say so.** Tables are namespaced `auth_` because `sessions` already holds learning sessions. | The numbering row was written when the auth backend was Better Auth, which would have created its own tables; passkeys in FastAPI need ours. Splitting into 009 + 010 would renumber nine downstream rows only just reconciled on 2026-08-23, for no benefit — both files ship in one slice, so there is no half-applied window. Shipping a wider migration than the table describes is exactly the failure #49 came from, so the table was corrected rather than quietly out-run. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: migration 009 is plain, non-idempotent DDL — no `IF NOT EXISTS` anywhere.** | PostgreSQL has no `IF NOT EXISTS` for `ADD CONSTRAINT`, so a half-guarded file fails on its second statement regardless; the guards would buy nothing but the false impression that a re-run is safe. The runner's per-file transaction and `schema_version` are what actually make a re-run impossible. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: `l1_pronunciation_seed` added at 009 rather than 017, nullable and inert until W14/W17.** Expected shape `{"contrasts": [...], "source": "...", "set_at": "..."}`. | #48's standing rule makes every `ALTER TABLE users` cost a view recreate against the production journal. Adding one inert nullable column to the migration that is already recreating the view is strictly cheaper and lower-risk than a second users-ALTER later for one column. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: `auth_email` carries a `CHECK (auth_email = lower(auth_email))`.** | The operator sets it by hand from psql and the service lowercases on both write and lookup. Without the constraint, an address stored with a capital letter is an account nobody can ever sign in to — a silent failure discovered on a phone. The constraint makes it a loud one at `UPDATE` time. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: every auth route is a plain `def`; challenges, sessions and rate-limit counters live in Postgres, never in process memory.** | The `llm`/`speech` rule (#7) exists because blocking work must not run on the event loop; a psycopg checkout and an ECDSA verification both block, so the rule's *reason* applies even though its named functions do not. And `english-api` runs `uvicorn --workers 2`: a module-level challenge cache works perfectly on one laptop process and fails roughly half of all real ceremonies, intermittently, only in production. An AST test bans module-level mutable state in both auth modules. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: the passkey-management routes are scoped to the session's user and return `404` — never `403` — for a credential that is not yours or an id that does not decode; the last-credential refusal is one transaction with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`.** | A `403` confirms the credential exists and turns an opaque id into an enumeration oracle. A count-then-delete lets two concurrent deletes each see two credentials and leave zero — self-lockout is the recovery case with no self-service way back, so it gets the transaction rather than a comment saying it is unlikely. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: `webauthn` is declared once, in `packages/core/pyproject.toml`; `requirements.txt`'s pre-existing `# packages/core` mirror gained a checked test rather than a fourth silent copy.** The deploy sequence gains **no new step** — `pip install -e packages/core` is already step 3 and installs it. | `core/passkeys.py` imports it, so it is core's dependency; declaring it only in `requirements.txt` would leave `core` importing something it does not declare, and anything installing `core` alone would `ImportError`. Two unchecked declarations of one dependency drift, which is how a version pin quietly becomes advisory — so `test_requirements_mirror_core_dependencies` asserts the two lists agree instead of the mirror being deleted for one of five entries. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: `py_webauthn` pinned `>=2.5,<3`; installed and verified version is `2.8.0`.** Every signature was read off the installed package before code was written. | The plan's signatures were written from memory. Two were wrong and would have failed at runtime: `VerifiedRegistration` has no `backup_eligible` field — it reports `credential_device_type` (`single_device`/`multi_device`) and `credential_backed_up`, which is what `auth_credentials` now stores; and `verify_registration_response` accepts a plain `dict`, so no separate `parse_*_json` step is needed. `>=2.5,<3` in a file and `2.8.0` in the venv are different facts, and six months from now the second is the one that explains a behaviour change. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: the test suite drives a programmable software authenticator (`tests/support/softauth.py`) that imports nothing from `core.passkeys`.** | A canned attestation/assertion blob bakes in its challenge, origin and sign count, so it cannot exercise challenge binding, origin binding or sign-count progression — the three things most worth testing. Borrowing the module's own encoders would make every verification test a proof that the module agrees with itself (CLAUDE.md §3 rule 5); a test asserts the fixture imports neither `core` nor `apps`. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: the `localStorage` ban is narrowed to permit theme preference only — one key, `"theme"`, in one file, `apps/web/lib/theme.ts` — and the theme provider is hand-rolled rather than `next-themes`.** `sessionStorage` stays banned outright. | A dependency would put the one permitted write inside `node_modules`, where the test cannot see it, which would make the narrowing meaningless. The test is narrowed, not deleted: `sessionStorage` banned everywhere; `localStorage` allowed in exactly one path; inside that path only the named constant, whose value is pinned to `"theme"`; plus a meta-test that feeds it four deliberate violations and requires each to fail. `globals.css` moved from `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` to a `.dark` class with a pre-paint inline script — leaving the media query in as well is what produces a toggle that visibly does nothing. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: `docs/TASKS-v3-web.md` marks W2 AGENT; it was run as PLAN.** | The AGENT marking was written before the domain was chosen. This slice applies the first migration to the live error journal, adds the first DNS records, terminates TLS on a host running a separate production service, and puts an HTTP surface on the internet. None of that is additive or self-contained, which is the AGENT criterion. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: `api.foundgrant.com` is DNS-only (grey cloud) on Cloudflare and Caddy owns its own certificate; `app.` is DNS-only for the same reason.** HSTS starts at `max-age=3600`, no `preload`, no `includeSubDomains`. | An orange-cloud record means Cloudflare terminates TLS and intercepts port 80, so HTTP-01 validation fails and Caddy would need an Origin certificate plus Full (strict) — a second TLS hop and a second certificate lifecycle for two users and no benefit. Caddy v2 adds no HSTS of its own, and a `__Host-` `Secure` cookie still leaves the first navigation downgradeable; the short initial max-age keeps a certificate or DNS mistake recoverable, and `preload` is effectively permanent on a domain that carries email forwarding. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: 009 is rehearsed against a scratch restore of production with `scripts/restore_from_r2.sh --keep` before it is applied to production, and the pre-migration backup is run by hand with `head-object` as the confirming read.** | **Why rehearse:** if 009 is going to fail it should fail against a copy, and W1c already built the machinery. **Why by hand:** the 04:00 UTC cron has never run unattended, so the backup step cannot lean on it. **Why `head-object`:** a read by exact key is strongly consistent; `list-objects-v2` is not (#73), and an empty first listing must not be read as a failed upload. **Why no script change:** `--keep` already exists (`restore_from_r2.sh:30, 35–37, 191–192, 279–283`) and line 198 already refuses to run when the scratch name equals `english_bot`. The plan had asked for a flag that was there all along — checking beat implementing. The rehearsal exports `DATABASE_URL` and unsets it afterwards, because exported values win over `.env` (#64). |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: the learners are on an iPhone 17 and a Samsung Galaxy A34 5G, not two iPhones — four platform assumptions corrected before the frontend was written.** | (1) The **separate cookie store is iOS-only**: Android installs a WebAPK that shares Chrome's cookies, so only the iPhone learner needs to sign in again inside the installed app. (2) **Chrome requires a registered service worker** with a fetch handler to install a real app rather than a bookmark shortcut, where Safari requires none — so the service worker is load-bearing for one learner, and the install check must be run against a deployed production build. (3) **WebKit's seven-day cap on script-writable storage is iOS-only** — the theme can be evicted on the iPhone, never on Android; the `HttpOnly` session cookie is unaffected on both. (4) **Metadata and acceptance checks were iPhone-shaped**: `appleWebApp`/`apple-touch-icon` serve one learner, the maskable icon and `theme_color` the other, and every "installed to the iPhone home screen" check is now per device. Passkeys sync through iCloud Keychain on one and Google Password Manager on the other; both are multi-device and nothing server-side changes. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: the frontend ships no hand-written base64url.** It uses the native `PublicKeyCredential.parseCreationOptionsFromJSON()` / `parseRequestOptionsFromJSON()` / `credential.toJSON()` bridge, feature-detected. | Hand-rolled binary conversion is the one piece of frontend logic here whose failure is **silent**: a mangled challenge or credential id surfaces as `NotAllowedError`, which is also what the browser throws when the learner cancels — so a bug looks exactly like someone changing their mind. Both devices are past the versions that ship the bridge (Safari 17.4+ / Chrome 119+, and Chrome updates independently of Android). A test fails the commit that reintroduces `atob`/`Uint8Array` in `lib/webauthn.ts`. This is also why **#67's recommendation to wait for W6 stands unchanged** — the untested part is component rendering, which is what W6 brings Vitest for. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: the sliding session cookie is re-set by the `get_current_user` dependency via an injected `Response`, not by each route.** Known asymmetry: if the route then raises, the row is extended and the client's cookie is not — the session is valid either way and the browser re-learns the expiry next request. | Putting the re-set in each route is exactly how "no route re-implements session lookup" gets broken by accident, and it is the kind of thing discovered halfway through implementation and solved the wrong way under time pressure. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W2: `GET /health/auth` is the only "am I signed in" route** — 200 with the session or literal `null`, never 401. No `GET /auth/me` was added. | A second route with the same job is a second place for session logic to drift. 200-with-null is also what the client guard needs: it distinguishes the two cases by body, not by status, and "am I signed in" stays one `curl` away (W0 risk R4). |
| 2026-08-23 | **W1c deploy — the W1 restructure is live on production and works.** `git pull` → `pip install -e packages/core` → `core.db status` reported **`Applied: 001–008, Pending: (none)`**: migrations 007 and 008 were **already applied** on Hetzner and only the S26c *code* had been undeployed. The systemd `ExecStart` still read `python -m app.main` and the service failed `status=1/FAILURE` until it was changed to `python -m apps.bot.main`; after `daemon-reload` the bot came up `active (running)`, loaded all twelve prompt templates from `core.PROMPTS_DIR`, registered its scheduler jobs and started polling. | Recorded as observed output, not expectation. Two assumptions in the W1c report were wrong and both matter: the deploy backlog was smaller than believed (the migrations were already there), and the *unit file* — not the code — was the thing that broke. `DEPLOYMENT.md` now warns that a pre-W1 unit fails this exact way, because the next person to pull onto a stale unit will see the same loop. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W1c: the R2 endpoint must carry the jurisdiction segment — `https://<account_id>.eu.r2.cloudflarestorage.com`.** | The bucket was created under the EU jurisdiction. A token scoped to an EU bucket signing against the *default* endpoint returns `AccessDenied` on **every** operation — `PutObject`, `ListObjectsV2` and `ListBuckets` alike — and nothing in the error names jurisdiction as the cause. It presents as a token permission problem and was initially misdiagnosed as one, costing several debugging cycles. Written into the `R2_ENDPOINT` comment in `.env.example` and into `DEPLOYMENT.md` so the misdiagnosis is not repeated. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W1c: `ALTER ROLE bot CREATEDB` is required on production**, and is recorded in `DEPLOYMENT.md` as a setup step. | `bot` owns `english_bot` but ownership does not permit creating a *new* database, and the restore drill creates and drops a scratch one on every run. `CREATEDB` lets a role create and drop its own databases; it is not superuser and widens access to no existing database. The alternative — running the drill as `postgres` — would demand superuser for a routine verification, which is strictly worse. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W1c: AWS CLI v2 installed from the official installer, not `apt`.** `/usr/local/bin/aws`, version 2.36.29, confirmed on `bot`'s PATH. | Ubuntu 24.04 has no `awscli` package in the default repositories, and the version available elsewhere is v1 — which the W1c write-up had assumed `apt` would provide. Install commands are now in `DEPLOYMENT.md` rather than a one-line `apt install` that does not work. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W1c: the restore drill closes #6, and the closing evidence is the row comparison — not the existence of an object in a bucket.** `errors` 27/27, `chunks` 29/29, `users` 3/3, `sessions` 43/44. `DRILL PASSED in 2s`; scratch database dropped. | `sessions` trailing live by one is the *correct* result: a dump should lag the live database by exactly the writes that happened after it was taken. An exact match on every table would have been the suspicious outcome — it would suggest the drill was comparing the live database to itself. This was the highest-ranked risk in the project, open since the Hetzner deploy on 2026-08-11. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W1c: retention verified across two consecutive backups** — uploads at 16:20 and 16:21, after which `list-objects-v2` returned **both** objects. | Retention deletes nothing inside the 14-day window. Worth recording because an intermediate diagnosis during the same session claimed retention was deleting live objects; it was wrong (see the new list-after-write issue) and the measurement is what settles it. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W1c: the freshness alarm was proved in both directions.** With the `english_bot/` prefix deleted: `R2Health(status='empty', should_alert=True, …)`. After a fresh backup: `R2Health(status='ok', should_alert=False, detail='Newest object … is 0.0h old (56176 bytes).')`. | An alarm that only ever fires is as useless as one that never does. One-directional evidence would have left the healthy state untested, which is the state it spends 364 days a year in. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W1c: `english-worker` and `english-api` were deliberately NOT installed.** `deploy/systemd/*.service` stays in the repo, uninstalled; both deploy at W2. | The worker is blocked by the job-table overlap it correctly detected (#69) — two processes through `streak_rollover` against one database is a data-integrity problem. The API would install cleanly but serves no routes until W2, and adding an idle service to a shared production host buys nothing today. |
| 2026-08-23 | **W1c: the kernel reboot (#32) was not needed and was not performed.** | The server is already running **6.8.0-138** — newer than the 6.8.0-137 the issue was written against — and `apt` reported *"Running kernel seems to be up-to-date. No services need to be restarted."* The box was rebooted at some point after the issue was filed. Nothing is outstanding. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **Cloudflare R2, not a Hetzner Storage Box.** | R2 is already required from W7 for generated audio, so it is not a new vendor relationship — it is one arriving early. More importantly it puts the backups behind a *different* vendor from the server: a compromised or suspended Hetzner account cannot take the database and its only off-site copy together. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **Bucket location Automatic → Eastern Europe; Standard storage class.** | Close to Nuremberg, inside the EU. Standard rather than Infrequent Access because the freshness check lists the newest object every hour and the restore drill reads one whole dump — IA's retrieval charges would cost more than the storage it saves at this volume. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **Account API token, not a User token, scoped to the single bucket.** | A User token dies with the user who created it. A backup that stops the day someone's account changes, and stops *silently*, is the exact failure this slice exists to close. Scoping to one bucket keeps the blast radius of the key on the server to the backups themselves. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **No client-IP restriction on the token.** | If Hetzner ever reassigns the address the backup breaks — and breaks silently, since the script would see an auth failure it cannot distinguish from a bad key. Bucket scoping is the safer boundary; an IP allow-list buys little against a key that is already file-mode-600 on one host. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **AWS CLI v2 (`aws s3` / `s3api` with `--endpoint-url`), not boto3.** | `scripts/backup.sh` runs from cron **without the venv** — that is the whole reason it is a shell script. boto3 would have meant either giving cron the venv or adding a Python dependency to `requirements.txt` for a shell-level concern, which CLAUDE.md §2 argues against. The CLI covers upload, listing, `head-object` verification and delete with no new project dependency, and Ubuntu 24.04 packages v2 in `apt`. The Python freshness check shells out to the *same* binary rather than importing a second client, so there is one tool, one credential path, and one thing to install. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **The script owns retention, not an R2 lifecycle rule.** | A lifecycle rule is invisible from the repo, untestable, and silently different on the Mac. `r2_prune_plan` is a pure function over a list of keys, so its policy is readable, diffable and covered by tests whose survivors are hand-written (CLAUDE.md §3 rule 5). |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **Retention reads the date embedded in the object key, not `LastModified`.** | The key carries the dump's own date, which is what the policy is about — an object re-uploaded after a failed run should not get a fresh 14 days. It also makes the decision testable with no clock and no network. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **The newest object is never pruned, whatever its age.** | If uploads have been broken for a month, the one surviving dump must not be deleted by its own retention rule. Retention exists to bound cost, not to guarantee the absence of a backup. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **Partial R2 configuration is a fatal error; zero configuration is an INFO skip.** | Nobody sets three of five keys on purpose. Treating a half-set group as "not configured" is precisely known issue #26 — something that looks unset skips quietly and the operator believes it ran. Zero keys stays a skip so the Mac works without credentials. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **`BACKUP_R2_REQUIRED` defaults to true (alarm) and a dev machine opts out.** | The slice asked for the server-versus-Mac decision to be made "by whether the five variables are present, not by hostname", but presence alone cannot tell an unconfigured server from an unconfigured laptop — they are the same state. A flag is a sixth variable and I would rather not have added one, so its polarity carries the argument: forgetting it on a laptop costs one extra alert, forgetting the opposite default costs silence in production. Recorded as issue #71 rather than hidden. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **The backup alarm throttles at 24 hours, composed in the callers rather than by widening `notify_operator`.** | A broken backup is a once-a-day fact; four alerts an hour would teach the operator to ignore the one alert that matters. `notify_operator` hard-codes the 15-minute cooldown the error handler wants, and adding a parameter to it means editing `packages/core/services/alerts.py`, which this slice was explicitly scoped out of. The throttle is therefore built in `apps/bot/scheduler.py` from `should_send_alert`, which is already public. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **Where the freshness alarm is actually delivered today — honestly.** | In the **bot** it goes to Telegram, to `OPERATOR_TELEGRAM_ID`, and the operator sees it on their phone without running anything. In the **worker** it lands in the journal at ERROR and stops there — `apps/worker` has no operator channel, the same gap `apps/api` has (issue #65). Since the worker unit is *not* being installed (issue #69), the delivered path today is the bot's, and it is a real alert rather than a log line. If the worker is ever installed without a sink, half of this alarm goes quiet again. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **The R2 secret is `repr=False` on `Settings` and on `R2Config`, passed in the child process's environment, and redacted out of any provider error text.** | `logger.info("%s", settings)` is one keystroke away in any file that holds a Settings object, and an argv is visible in `ps` while a child's environment is not. The redaction is defence in depth for the one path where a credential could arrive from outside and be forwarded into an alert body. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **`english-api` unit written for install; `english-worker` unit written and deliberately left uninstalled.** | The two job tables overlap on `streak_rollover`, `monthly_freeze_reset`, `heartbeat` and `backup_freshness`. Two processes running `streak_rollover` against one database is a data-integrity problem, and the slice's own instruction was to stop and report rather than install. Recorded as issue #69 with a test that fails when the overlap changes. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **Unit files live in `deploy/systemd/` in the repo, not only as fenced blocks in `DEPLOYMENT.md`.** | The unit that runs should be the unit that was reviewed. A markdown code block gets retyped, and the difference between the two is discovered at 04:00. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **`scripts/restore_from_r2.sh` has no `--force` and cannot name the live database.** | Unlike `scripts/restore.sh`, this is meant to be run casually and often — that is the whole point of a drill. A flag that can overwrite the error journal does not belong in something you run casually. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **The restore drill was not performed at build time, and `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` recorded that rather than a template.** **Superseded the same day by the deploy** — the drill ran against production and that section now holds real numbers. | The drill needed the R2 credentials and the production database; both live on the server and the build session had no access to either. Writing a plausible-looking table of row counts would have read exactly like evidence, which is worse than an empty section. Keeping the empty section is what made the real numbers legible as real when they arrived. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **`localStorage` ban lifted for the theme preference only.** | The human wants a light/dark toggle; it arrives at W2, which owns the shell and dark mode. Everything else — session state, learner content, progress — stays server-side. The original ban came from a rule about throwaway previews and was never a product decision. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1c: **The domain is `foundgrant.com`** (already owned, registrar Namecheap, nameservers moved to Cloudflare 2026-08-23). `app.foundgrant.com` → Vercel, `api.foundgrant.com` → Hetzner behind Caddy. | Already owned, so no new purchase and no waiting on propagation at W2. The existing Namecheap email-forwarding MX records and the SPF TXT record were imported into Cloudflare intact and **must not be deleted** — mail for the domain stops the moment they are. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **`test_vocabulary_due_and_anki` now reads `CURRENT_DATE + 1` from the database after the insert** and passes that as `now`, instead of a frozen date or a changed `insert_chunks`. | The insert writes `next_review = CURRENT_DATE + 1` **server-side**; any date the test names itself is a second opinion about what day it is. Reading it back asks the same server the same question (CLAUDE.md §3 rule 6). Order matters: read *after* the insert, so a run that crosses midnight compares against the earlier day and stays due. Closes #61. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **the `assert_path_outside_repo` tests derive the repo root from `tests/`** (`Path(__file__).parents[1]`), never from `repo_root()`, and there are now five cases: under `packages/`, under `scripts/`, a direct child of the root, the root itself, and `/tmp` accepted. | The old single test built its "inside" fixture from the function under test, so when W1 left `repo_root()` one directory too shallow the guard was broken and the test stayed green (CLAUDE.md §3 rule 5). `scripts/x.sql` and the direct-child case are the two that a `parents[2]` root accepts — **verified by re-introducing the bug: three of the five fail, then pass again when it is reverted.** Closes #63. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **`core.db.current_schema_version()` was added** rather than putting a query in the route or calling `core.db.status()` from it. | SQL in `apps/api` fails `test_no_sql_outside_services`, deliberately. `status()` opens its own connection *and* runs `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS schema_version` — a health endpoint a monitor polls every minute must not run DDL. The new function is read-only and uses the shared pool, so `/health` fails exactly when a real request would. `core/db.py` already owns `schema_version` and is already the boundary test's named exemption. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **`GET /health` answers 503, not 500, when the database is unreachable**, and catches that one exception itself. | "The service is up, its dependency is not" is a different fact from "this route crashed", and it is the one an uptime check acts on. The generic 500 handler stays the rule for everything else; this is the one probe that is allowed to know why it failed. No exception text crosses the wire either way. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **`GET /health/auth` has no `response_model`** and returns the resolved session verbatim (`null` today). | A pydantic model for a session that Better Auth has not defined yet would be a guess, and the guess would be copied into `lib/api.ts` and then into every screen. `schemas/__init__.py` says W2 adds `Session` and types this route then. The route already reads `get_current_user`, and a test overrides that dependency to prove it renders what the dependency returns — otherwise the `null` proves nothing. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **`http://localhost:3000` is a constant in `apps/api/main.py`, not configuration**, and `allowed_origins()` drops any origin containing `*` or missing a scheme even though `core.config` already rejects those. | `pnpm dev` always serves on 3000; putting it in `.env` means every developer's file drifts and the deploy has two origins to get right instead of one. The second filter is belt-and-braces: this list is what the middleware trusts, and "the validator upstream catches it" is how a credentialed wildcard ships. `allow_credentials=True` from day one so CORS is exercised in the shape W2's cookie needs. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **the API's operator alert is a logging stub** (`send_operator_alert` → `logger.error`), reusing `format_alert` and `should_send_alert` for the text and the throttle. | The bot's channel is `context.bot`, which lives in the bot process; borrowing it means importing Telegram into `apps/api`, which CLAUDE.md §2 forbids. Sharing the throttle file means a burst of API 500s cannot flood a channel the bot's throttle does not cover. Recorded as #65 so the missing channel is a tracked gap rather than an assumption. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **the worker takes `RUNTIME_DIR/worker.lock`, derived from `INSTANCE_LOCK_FILE`, not a new environment variable** — and writes `RUNTIME_DIR/worker.log`, not the bot's log. | The bot and the worker must be able to run at the same time, so they cannot share a lock; and a second env var is a second thing to forget on a deploy. Separate log files because `configure_logging` installs a `RotatingFileHandler`, and two processes rotating one file lose whichever one's lines are in flight when it rolls. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **the worker reads the heartbeat file and never writes it.** | The file means "a scheduled job succeeded recently", and until W20 every delivery job is still in `apps/bot`. A worker that touched it would keep it fresh while the bot lay dead and the staleness alarm would never fire again. The writing moves here when the deliveries do. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **both `monthly_freeze_reset` and `monthly_reset` are registered**, though the second calls the first. | They are the two units ARCHITECTURE-v3 §7 names, and both are idempotent per user per local day (`freeze_reset_on`; the `fossil_sweep` session guard), so the overlap costs one extra no-op query per 15 minutes. Collapsing them now would mean choosing which name survives while the bot's own scheduler still registers a third variant. Tracked as #66, resolved at W20. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **`build_scheduler()` returns a `BlockingScheduler` with jobs added but not started**, and the job table is a `tuple[Job, ...]` of plain data. | Registration is the thing that broke in v2 (#54): five tests passed while asserting only predicates, so all five would pass against a worker that registers nothing. A table that can be inspected without starting a process is what makes "assert every job by name and trigger" a one-line test. The expected table in `test_worker.py` is written out by hand rather than imported, or it would assert that `JOBS` equals itself. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **the plain-`def` route rule is enforced by AST**, and `await asyncio.to_thread(llm.chat, ...)` is explicitly *not* flagged. | A rule that also flags the sanctioned escape teaches people to hide the call instead of moving it off the loop. Because `apps/api` has no such route yet, the detector would be inert — so `test_the_plain_def_detector_actually_detects` runs it over an offending and a compliant fixture and asserts both results (CLAUDE.md §3 rule 4). |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **the new boundary test bans `apscheduler` and `telegram` in `apps/api`, and cross-imports between `apps/api`, `apps/worker` and `apps/bot`** — and deliberately does **not** ban `apps.*` inside `core`. | The API runs several uvicorn workers, each a whole process; a scheduler imported there fires every job once per worker (W0 risk R3). Widening the same test to `core → apps` would close #60, but it would fail on pre-existing code (`access_control.delivery_lister_ids`) and the pressure would be to weaken the test. That inversion gets its own slice. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **`apps/web` uses `@ducanh2912/next-pwa`, not `next-pwa`.** | ARCHITECTURE-v3 §2 says "`next-pwa` / Workbox". The original has had no release since 2022 and declares support only to Next 13; this is the maintained fork of the same Workbox setup, with Next 14/15 in its peer range. Same mechanism, still built. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **dark mode follows `prefers-color-scheme` with no toggle**, and shadcn's `.dark` class variant was rewritten as a media query. | A toggle has to remember its setting, and the two places to remember it in a browser are `localStorage` and a cookie. The slice bans the first outright and the second belongs to W2's session. Following the system is also what a phone user expects at dusk. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: the web shell's visual direction — **warm paper / deep teal-slate, one teal accent, Fraunces headings over Geist text, `max-w-lg` at every breakpoint, and no red anywhere in the palette.** | `/mnt/skills/public/frontend-design/SKILL.md` **does not exist in this environment**, so the direction was chosen rather than read, and is written down in `apps/web/README.md` so W6 onwards has something to be consistent with. Phone-width even on a desktop: both learners use a phone, and a second responsive layout is a second design to maintain for a screen nobody opens. No red because "never guilt" (CLAUDE.md §4) is easier to hold to when the colour for failure is not in the theme. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **the `/health` check on the Today screen runs in the browser, not in a server component.** | A server component would call the API over loopback and prove nothing about CORS — which is the half that breaks on a phone (W0 risk R4). It is also the only thing on the shell that can fail, so it is the only thing worth rendering a state for. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **the PWA manifest is `app/manifest.ts` (typed) rather than a hand-kept `public/manifest.json`**, and the four icons are generated by a script into `public/icons/`. | A wrong `display` or a missing icon size becomes a build error instead of a home-screen tile that turns out to be a browser shortcut. The icons are geometric and generated because there is no design asset yet and a placeholder that says "replace me" never gets replaced; `test_web_shell.py` asserts every file the manifest names actually exists. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **the web shell's invariants are tested from the Python suite** (`tests/test_web_shell.py`) — no `localStorage`/`sessionStorage`, four nav items each with a page, one button on Today, manifest icons present, API base URL from the environment. | `pytest -q` is the only suite this repo runs; a second test runner nobody invokes is not coverage. The storage scan strips comments first and proves it does so, because the comment explaining why the app avoids `localStorage` contains the word — the grep-versus-parse trap the boundary tests already avoid. Adding a real JS test runner is #67, and belongs to W6 when components exist to render. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **`WEB_ORIGIN` is validated in `core.config`** (scheme required, no trailing slash) rather than accepted as free text. | CORS compares origins literally: `https://app.example.com/` never matches anything a browser sends, and the failure appears on a phone as a blank screen with a console message nobody is reading. Failing at startup is cheaper. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **`requirements.txt` is now grouped by which application needs each dependency**, and declares `APScheduler` explicitly. | It arrived transitively through `python-telegram-bot[job-queue]`; `apps/worker` imports it directly, and a dependency you rely on but do not declare disappears the day the transitive path changes. `httpx` is declared for the same reason — it is how the API tests reach the app through its real ASGI transport. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1b: **Next.js is pinned at 15.5.23**, not the current 16. | ARCHITECTURE-v3 §2 says Next.js 15, and a shell is the wrong slice to take a major-version bump in — the first thing it would break is the PWA plugin, which is the one piece here that is version-sensitive. |

They had to be in core before that commit could touch them. The omission was in the enumeration, not the intent. |
| 2026-08-23 | W1: **`app/config.py` → `packages/core/config.py`** and **`app/scheduler.py` → `apps/bot/scheduler.py`**, though neither appeared in the W0 §5.2 mv list. | Both are named by later commits in the same plan (`app.config` → `core.config` in the commit-3 rewrite; `apps/bot/scheduler.py` in commit 5). Leaving them behind would have left an `app/` package holding two files nothing could import. |

They live in `apps/bot/anki_delivery.py` and `apps/bot/alerts.py` and die at W22 with the rest of the bot. |

| 2026-08-14 | S24b: name skipped words in the Telegram reply; log count + reason category only | Sender owns the content and needs the detail. Logs must not hold vocabulary (PRD §10). Cap + “and N more” avoids a wall of text. |
| 2026-08-14 | S24b: register balance + everyday sense as prompt instructions only | `work_domain` was colouring nearly every sentence. No unit test can judge register — next live import is the real check. Prefer plain sense over idiom (“a notch above”). |
| 2026-08-14 | S24a: generate example sentences rather than reject sentence-less Trancy files | Real Trancy vocabulary export has no sentence column and cannot be configured to add one. Chunks without `full_sentence` cannot feed S7 Anki cloze, S16 shadowing, or S7a gap-fill. Cost: sentences are generated, not mined — the word is no longer tied to the scene where it was met (memorability loss). Slang CSV (real examples) stays the better source. |
| 2026-08-14 | S24a: send Translation glosses into the LLM for sense selection | Multi-sense words (`notch`, `tier`) need the Persian gloss; bare word makes the model pick a sense at random. Prompt: use gloss for sense only, everyday sense when several listed, English output only. |
| 2026-08-14 | S24a: `Translation` column name is the structural never-share marker | Native-language meanings by construction. A column name (not a separate flag) is what the classifier already sees — a flag could drift from the header. Vocabulary never reaches Share / `shared_content` code. |
| 2026-08-14 | S24a: batch up to 40 words per LLM call; sequential batches; whole-file abort on failure | One call per row would stall the handler and inflate cost. Cap keeps long Persian glosses + sentence outputs inside a sane `max_tokens`. Any batch failure → zero rows (never a half-import). |
| 2026-08-14 | S24a: sender-only is structure, not a Share default | Mis-tap must be impossible — no Share keyboard, no ledger rows. Any user (incl. Morkytė) imports to self only. |
| 2026-08-14 | S24a: real header `{word,phonetic,translation,date}` vs S15a guess | S15a inferred Word+Translation plus a sentence-like column. Real export is four columns, no sentence. Trancy-legacy now **requires** a sentence-like column structurally (not order-only vs vocabulary). |
| 2026-08-14 | S24a: folder path refuses vocabulary (WARNING → `failed/`); Telegram is the acceptance path | Calling the LLM inside `watch_poll` is known issue #7. `WATCH_DIR` is unset in prod — do not plant that failure in a dormant path. |
| 2026-08-14 | S24a: generate with no write transaction held; then insert→send→commit | Pool `max=5`; holding a connection across a 40-sentence generation would starve other handlers. |
| 2026-08-14 | S24: fan-out one row per approved user — not nullable `chunks.user_id` + join table | Every consumer (S7a ladder, `due_chunks`, Anki, `/stats`, quiz) assumes `user_id`-scoped rows; a shared catalog would force a review-state join and refactor S7a/S7/quiz for two users. Fan-out also gives each person their own `next_review`. Cost is duplicated rows; storage is not a constraint at this scale. |
| 2026-08-14 | S24: English-only shared content — slang Share opt-in; Trancy/LR never auto-share | Operator Trancy exports carry Persian meanings; User B is Lithuanian — Persian in her queue/Anki is noise. Slang CSV is English-to-English (safe by construction) but still requires explicit Share / Just me. “Share everything the operator imports” rejected. |
| 2026-08-14 | S24: convergent backfill — Save (soft-fail) + both approve/re-approve handlers + reconcile at fan-out top | One-shot hooks lose the library forever if they throw. Soft-fail never breaks onboarding; weekly slang CSV repairs gaps. Call sites are idempotent via `shared_content_deliveries`. |
| 2026-08-14 | S24: skip-owned still writes a delivery (`skipped_owned`) | Without it the ledger permanently disagrees with reality and every backfill re-checks forever. Copy insert + delivery in one transaction. |
| 2026-08-14 | S24: phonetic appended to `meaning` as `… (/ipa/)` — no schema column | No consumer today (S16 TTS uses `full_sentence`). |
| 2026-08-14 | S24: chunk ledger first-write-wins; book ledger refreshes title/items | Same word with a new example must not rewrite everyone’s mid-review card. Books refresh because OCR can improve a unit; never overwrite `studied_at`/`created_at` (S6a/S11). |
| 2026-08-14 | S24: `SHARED_BOOK_SLUGS` env (normalised match) + INFO when operator slug stays personal | Not a hardcoded Python list. Silent non-share is indistinguishable from a broken config. |
| 2026-08-14 | S24: whole-header slang signature (exact five columns) — not token presence | Token matching would let a Trancy file look like slang and fan Persian meanings out. Mutual exclusion with Trancy/LR; unknown → reject. |
| 2026-08-14 | S24: Share is CallbackQuery only (no MessageHandler / ConversationHandler); orphan `share:` warm line | Free-text ConversationHandler states twice killed M2. Restart between file and tap clears `user_data`. |
| 2026-08-14 | S24: backfill called from access_request + admin handlers — not `access_control.approve_access` | Avoids circular import with the delivery-lister registry. Both grant paths must wire backfill. |

CSV upload via Telegram (S15b) is now the only import route — exactly why it was built. **S15a is effectively dormant in production.** |
| 2026-08-11 | `BACKUP_OFFSITE_DIR` unset on the server | Google Drive is unreachable from the host; no off-site destination configured yet. Local dumps land on the same disk as the database (known issue #6). |

| 2026-08-11 | S18d: open `/start` was a live hole on a public server | ARCHITECTURE §7 allowed `/start` for anyone; full onboarding + LLM spend. Closed before Phase 5 tenancy. |

| 2026-08-11 | S18d: `OPERATOR_TELEGRAM_ID` unset → store pending, loud log, tell requester access closed | Silent drop hides misconfiguration; storing lets the operator catch up when configured. |

Approval precedes wizard so mid-onboarding allowlist is redundant and fragile on restart. Gate is load-bearing — surface regression test mandatory. |
| 2026-08-11 | S18d: decline cap = 2 operator DMs | Re-request after decline stays allowed (mistaken decline lockout is worse); uncapped cycles spam the operator from an unauthenticated stranger. |
| 2026-08-11 | S18d: `on_error` soft-reply-to-strangers closed by the gate | Unapproved updates never reach group-0 handlers. Residual only if the gate itself throws (DB down) — fail-soft under outage, not an access hole. |
| 2026-08-11 | S18d: `/admin` omitted from `setMyCommands`, `/help`, `/guide` | Advertising it teaches strangers the command exists; silent ignore for non-operators is the security UX. |

## Known issues

| # | Issue | Severity | Slice | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 121 | **The blind solver is BLIND TO THE AUDIO, so for `listening_gap` it sees strictly less than the learner does.** PRD §4.3 defines the gate as a second model call that sees *"only what the learner will see"*. `visible_projection` adds nothing for this type — *"their content is audio"* (projection.py:104) — so the probe is handed `{"item_type": "listening_gap", "prompt_text": "She ___ like coffee these days"}` and asked which answers a teacher would have to accept, **while the learner hears the sentence**. In text that slot admits `doesn't`, `does not` and `might not`; with the audio it admits one. So the probe is solving a strictly harder problem than the item poses, and the multi-acceptability it reports may be an artefact of what it was denied rather than a defect in the item. **This is the W6 run's fifth rejection and it is NOT #115**: `listening_gap` passed naturalness (its judged string is the clean transcript) and failed `_probe_and_repair` at `not_recoverable` after the `first_letter_length` rung — the only rung available, since the fixture carries no `definition` or `l1_gloss`. If confirmed, **no `listening_gap` item can pass a text-only probe as authored**, and the fix is to the gate's design for this type — give the probe the transcript as heard, or gate the type another way — **not to the fixture**. W5a added the probe to this type precisely because it had only ever been audio-gated; the gate it gained may be one it cannot satisfy. Arm C of `judge_observe --live` tests it directly. | high | W5a → W5b → the fix slice | ⬜ open — arm C unrun; the structural claim is read off `projection.py` and the W6 verdict |
| 120 | **`JUDGE_BATCH = 20` is declared and never used, so W10 pays one model call per item where the design says one per twenty.** `gates.py:76` defines it, `judge_naturalness`'s own docstring says *"batched up to `JUDGE_BATCH` per call"*, and `validate` calls `judge_naturalness([sentence])` — **one sentence at a time**, at gates.py:400. The function is genuinely batch-capable: it numbers the sentences, parses a verdict per row and aligns them positionally. Nothing calls it that way. **This is a real W10 budget line, not a tidiness note**: `assign_daily` validates a batch of candidates per learner per day, and the naturalness gate is the second-cheapest of four, so a 20× call multiplier on it is paid on every candidate that survives the mechanical rules — including every candidate later discarded by the probe. The docstring and the W5 slice note both describe batching that does not happen, so anyone sizing W10 from the record will size it wrong. **Not fixed here**: batching changes what request goes to the provider, and W5b's whole discipline is that it does not modify the thing it is observing. | medium | W5 → W5b → W10 | ⬜ open — declared, documented, never called |
| 119 | **The gates ask the model for diagnostics and throw them away, which is the only reason W5b had to exist.** `item_naturalness.txt` ends *"Use one of these for `reason`: ok, slack, textbook, stilted, jargon, written"*, and `judge_naturalness` reads `row["natural"]` and discards `row["reason"]` (gates.py:327-335). `validate` then records the bare string `"unnatural"` in `ValidationReport.naturalness`, which is what `seed_fixtures --live` printed and all a human could see. **The model was asked to say why, said why, and the answer was dropped one line before anything could read it** — so a `high` issue was opened that a stored word would have answered, and a slice was spent recovering it. `item_probe.txt`'s `confidence` field is the same class: requested in the prompt, never read by `_candidates` (gates.py:209-222). **The fix is small and belongs with the #115 fix**: carry the reason into `ValidationReport.naturalness` so it lands in `items.validation`, where migration 012 already stores the record. The cost of leaving it is measured: the next unexplained rejection costs another investigation. | medium | W5 → W5b → the fix slice | ⬜ open — the reason is parsed and dropped at gates.py:331 |
| 118 | **`match_pairs` cannot show its correct answer, and W6a could only stop it pretending to.** Its correct answer is a bijection in `items.payload.pairs`, which `visible_projection` withholds because it **is** the answer, and `AnswerOutcome` carries no field for it — the answer route returns `canonical` and nothing else, which is NULL for this type by the `items_answer_present_iff_type_has_one` CHECK. So nothing on the client has the pairing. W6a fixed the half it could: the verdict box no longer promises an answer it cannot produce (#112) and the component says so plainly. **The learner still does not find out the correct pairing**, which on a wrong answer means the item taught nothing. The fix is the answer route returning the pairing **after grading** — not a projection leak, for the same reason `canonical` is not one — and that is an API change W6a was explicitly barred from making. `speak_answer` has the same NULL canonical but does not need this: an open-production task has no single answer to show. | medium | W6a → W7 | ⬜ open — needs the answer route to return the mapping post-grading |
| 117 | **The startup log line W6's deployment steps named as evidence does not appear in `journalctl`.** `apps/api/main.py` logs `API built origins=%s routes=%s` at INFO from `create_app()`, and the W6 runbook cited it as the evidence that step 7 (restart) had registered the item routes. On the 2026-08-25 deploy it was not there. The routes were proven live another way — a `401` from `GET /items` in the access log, which is step 8 — so nothing was actually unverified, but **a deployment step must not cite evidence that does not exist**: the next person follows it, does not find the line, and cannot tell a missing log line from a failed restart. Likely a log-level or handler question under systemd rather than a missing call. Either make the line appear or correct the step to cite the 401. | low | W6 → W7 | ⬜ open — evidence cited, evidence absent |
| 116 | **The per-type leak assertions are weakest exactly where the answer is on screen.** For the six types in `ANSWER_ON_SCREEN` (the five `tap` types plus `speak_repeat`) `test_no_response_body_carries_the_answer` skips, and `test_the_discriminator_stays_hidden_where_the_answer_is_on_screen` checks **JSON key names only** — so `word_bank_order`'s correct permutation and `match_pairs`' mapping are asserted by the absence of a key, not by the absence of their value. Only `error_spot` gets a value check. **Tiles arriving pre-sorted into the correct order would pass both tests**, and the item would be unanswerable-by-being-trivial rather than by leaking a field. Verified not happening on production 2026-08-25 — the tiles came shuffled — but nothing enforces it. `checks._word_bank`'s `bank_already_ordered` failure covers the generator's side; nothing covers the serving side. | medium | W6 → W7 | ⬜ open — key-level assertions where value-level are needed |
| 115 | **The first evidence about the naturalness gate from outside its own test suite, and it is not reassuring. Accept rate 6/11.** The W6 seeding run put the eleven committed `tests/fixtures/items/valid.json` fixtures through the real validator on production 2026-08-25. **Five were rejected**: `mcq`, `cloze_cued`, `error_spot` and `collocation_pick` as **`unnatural` with zero repairs**, and `listening_gap` as **`not_recoverable` after a `first_letter_length` repair**. Exactly one of two things is true and they need different fixes. **Either the naturalness judge over-rejects** — in which case W10's `assign_daily` burns model calls generating items a person would call perfectly fine, and the generator's yield is far worse than anyone has budgeted for — **or `valid.json` contains items that are not valid**, in which case ~1,340 tests have been passing over fixtures the gate itself rejects, and every assertion built on them describes something that could never ship. The `unnatural` verdicts are the sharper half: those items are `"I ___ to the shops yesterday."` and `"She ___ a photo of us at the beach."`, which read as ordinary English. **Eleven items, one sample each, of a stochastic system** — this licenses no claim about the gate's rate in either direction, and it does license *look at this before W10 depends on it*. **CAUSE IDENTIFIED 2026-08-25 (W5b), and it is neither branch.** The gate judges `checks.sentence_of(item)`, which falls through to `prompt_text` for `mcq`, `cloze_cued` and `collocation_pick` — **the gapped stem** — and returns the joined tiles for `error_spot`, **the sentence with its deliberate error still in it**. `item_naturalness.txt` never mentions gaps, exercises or learners and instructs the model to reject anything *"nobody actually phrases that way"*, so **the judge is correct to reject a string with a blank in it**. The judge does not over-reject and `valid.json` is not invalid; the defect is in what `sentence_of` hands the judge, a third possibility this issue did not contain. The split is a **perfect partition, eleven of eleven**: every `unnatural` verdict is a defective judged string, every clean-prose string passed, and the fifth rejection — `listening_gap` — passed naturalness and failed at the probe, which is **#121** and a separate defect. **There is no threshold**: the verdict is a raw boolean and a missing row fails open, so the gate cannot be tuned looser even if someone wanted to. **Consequence for W10, and it is worse than a low yield**: the gapped types are `mcq`, `cloze_cued`, `collocation_pick` and `listening_gap`, the backbone of the drill set, and each would be rejected, regenerated and rejected again for the same structural reason — **four item types that can never be generated**, with a loop that never converges. **6/11 must be deleted from W10's assumptions: it measures a bug, not a yield.** Recommended fix, for the human to rule on: give the judge prose — a `judged_sentence()` that fills the gap and corrects the error tile — leaving `sentence_of` its current job for the mechanical rules. **Not fixed in W5b, by design**, and the counterfactual (does the judge accept the filled sentence?) is arm B of `python -m core.items.judge_observe --live` and is **unrun**. | high | W5a → W5b → the fix slice | ⬜ open — **cause identified, not fixed**. | 65 | **`apps/api` has no operator alert channel.** An unhandled 500 is formatted and throttled through `core.services.alerts` and then written to the log at ERROR instead of delivered. On the server that means `journalctl -u english-api`, which nobody is watching. The bot's channel cannot be borrowed without importing Telegram into the API (CLAUDE.md §2). W1c widened this: `apps/worker` has the same gap, and the W1c backup alarm runs in both processes — in the bot it reaches Telegram, in the worker it reaches the journal and stops there. While the worker unit is uninstalled (#69) the bot's copy is the one that actually alerts, so the alarm does reach a human today; if the worker is ever installed without a sink, half of it goes quiet again. **Raised at W2, and retargeted: the API now serves real public traffic at `api.foundgrant.com`, so an unhandled 500 on a route the learners depend on lands in `journalctl -u english-api` and nowhere else. Not fixed in W2 (out of scope); the interim signal is `curl /health` and `curl /health/auth`.** A separate pointer issue was deliberately *not* opened for this — a row whose content is "this is #65 getting worse" carries nothing of its own and would be carried forward forever. **Confirmed at the W2 closeout, and now the highest-priority open infrastructure issue.** The plan predicted this would "bite for the first time here"; the access log settles it. Within four minutes of certificate issuance the API was being **continuously scanned** — `/.env`, `/.git/config`, `/wp-login.php`, `/actuator/env`, `/telescope/requests`, cpanel/WHM paths, a `POST /graphql` sweep, from a dozen addresses. All 404, nothing exposed. But the surface is public, permanently probed, and **every 500 it produces lands in a journal nobody reads**. **W3 makes this worse, not better:** `POST /correct` is the first route that spends money per request and the first that writes to the error journal, so its failures are both expensive and silent. **W3 raised it again and made it concrete:** `POST /correct` now calls `llm.py` on a public, continuously-probed API, so a provider outage or a malformed response is both **expensive** and **silent** — it answers 503 to the learner and writes one WARNING to a journal nobody reads. Not fixed in W3, correctly: it is infrastructure, not correction. But the slice that fixes it now inherits a route that spends money per call rather than two health checks. **As of the 08:18–08:20 UTC restart on 2026-08-24 that route is live in production, so the cost of the missing sink is being incurred rather than predicted.** | **high** | W1b → W1c → W2 → **W3** | ⬜ open — a real sink (Sentry, or an HTTP call to the bot). That is exactly what CLAUDE.md §3 rule 2 says a mock cannot do — verify a provider contract. The endpoint-jurisdiction failure (`AccessDenied` on every operation against the non-EU endpoint) is a second thing no stub could have surfaced. **2026-08-24 adds a third mode the stub could not exercise: upload and listing have now run *unattended*, on a schedule, with no operator present** — the 04:00 cron dump and the 08:09:30 freshness verdict. #70 stays open on its own terms: `head-object` response shape, checksum behaviour and token scope are still assumptions for every path the stub still stands in for. | **high** | W1c | ⬜ open — upload, listing and `head-object` have now run for real; keep open for the paths the stub still stands in for, and treat any new argv change as needing one real call |
| 71 | **`BACKUP_R2_REQUIRED` is a sixth variable in a group of five.** The W1c prompt asked for the server-versus-Mac decision to be made "by whether the five variables are present, not by hostname", but presence alone cannot distinguish an unconfigured server from an unconfigured laptop — both look identical. The flag is the smallest honest answer and its default direction is safe (unset = alarm), but it is one more thing in `.env`. If a better production signal appears, replace it. | low | W1c | ⬜ open — flagged deliberately, not an oversight |
| 72 | **The Mac's `.env` has no `BACKUP_R2_REQUIRED=0` yet.** Running the bot locally will now raise one "R2 unconfigured" alert a day to the operator id. Harmless and by design, but the line should be added to the Mac's `.env` (not `.env.example`, which documents the production default). **See #85: the Mac turned out to be *mis*configured rather than unconfigured, so the daily local alarm this row predicted arrives as an `AccessDenied` ERROR about a real bucket. `BACKUP_R2_REQUIRED=0` is one of #85's two resolutions — the one that accepts the laptop verifies nothing.** | low | W1c | ⬜ open — one line, human's local file (or fix the endpoint per #85) |
| 73 | **R2 list-after-write consistency lag.** An object uploaded at 16:14 on 2026-08-23 and successfully *downloaded* by the restore drill at 16:15 did **not** appear in `list-objects-v2` several minutes later: the freshness check reported `status='empty'` for a bucket that provably held it, and a subsequent listing showed the object had been there all along. The object was never lost and retention was never at fault — **an intermediate diagnosis that retention was deleting live objects was wrong**, and is recorded here so it is not re-derived later. Consequence: the freshness check has no tolerance for listing lag and **can produce a false alarm if it runs shortly after an upload.** Harmless at the 04:00 UTC cron, which has hours of slack; it bites when the alarm is being tested by hand — **and the slack prediction is now confirmed: the 2026-08-24 08:09:30 UTC check, run 4.2 hours after the upload, returned `ok`.** The fix path and the by-hand false-alarm risk are unchanged. Fix path: treat `empty` as inconclusive when the newest *local* dump is very recent, or re-list once after a short delay before alerting. **Not fixed in W1c** — the slice was recording, not coding. **Not fixed in W2 either, by instruction: the W2 deploy runbook works around it** by confirming the pre-migration backup with `head-object` (a read by exact key, strongly consistent) and treating an empty `list-objects-v2` as inconclusive rather than as a failed upload. | medium | W1c | ⬜ open — fix in a later slice; do not test the alarm within minutes of a backup |

Verified in all three directions: unset refuses, empty string refuses, a valid value builds. |

The consequence is not untidiness: Telegram hands each update to exactly one poller, so a learner's message can be consumed by a laptop and written to the **Mac's development database**, or dropped when the loser raises. This is the same failure class as the W1b disclosure already on this record, arriving through a different door — there the wrong `.env` was read, here the wrong host wins the update. That is exactly the shape here. **Two acceptable resolutions, and they are not equivalent:** correct `R2_ENDPOINT` on the Mac to the `.eu.` form so local runs genuinely verify something, **or** take #72's `BACKUP_R2_REQUIRED=0` and accept that the laptop checks nothing. gating cannot be middleware — the cookie boundary), **#75** (iOS installed-app
cookie store; iPhone only), **#76** (`--proxy-headers` or the rate limiter
buckets everyone together). **#77** and **#78** were opened and closed inside the
slice, and **#64 closed with #78** — `load_settings()` now takes an explicit
dotenv path, so the suite no longer means different things on different
machines. **#65 raised to `high` and retargeted to W2** — the API now serves real

alone**: its `apps.bot` imports are function-local, so `access_control.is_approved`
is callable from `apps/api` with no Telegram exposure, and it did not force a
duplicated predicate in the auth path. **#69** still blocks the worker install;
**#73** is worked around in the W2 runbook, not fixed; **#45** still belongs to
W3; **#67**'s "wait for W6" recommendation stands, because the frontend ships no
hand-written base64url.

## File inventory

Cursor: keep this current so a fresh chat knows what exists without reading the repo.

**What the W3 ticks rest on.** The `packages/core/services/correction.py`, `packages/core/prompt_rules.py` and `apps/bot/handlers/*` rows are evidenced by the startup log line naming `core.services.correction` and by the 08:18–08:20 UTC restart. The `apps/web/*` rows rest on **the human's report** of the `/write` check — no build or deploy log for the web app is in this record.

| **W5b — new** |  |  |
| `packages/core/items/judge_observe.py` | **Human-run, dry-run by default, never an acceptance check** — the same category as `verify.py` and `seed_fixtures.py`. Observes what the naturalness judge is actually asked and what it actually answers, including the one-word `reason` `judge_naturalness` discards (#119). Three arms: **A** exactly `sentence_of`, **B** the same sentence with the gap closed and the error tile corrected, **C** two public `probe_acceptable` calls for `listening_gap` with no audio gate (#121). Wraps the existing `gates._chat` seam so the real judge runs and the verdict and reason come from the same call; **modifies nothing**. Predictions and branch rules are in the docstring, written before the run, and `--live` evaluates them itself. Imports no `core.services`, opens no connection, contacts no host. | 🟡 |
| `tests/test_items_judge_observe.py` | 26 offline tests, zero model calls. The load-bearing one asserts arm A is **byte-identical** to `checks.sentence_of` across all eleven fixtures, so a later change to `sentence_of` breaks the suite rather than silently invalidating the finding. Also pins the 10/4/6 partition, that the shim restores the seam on the exception path, that a probe-shaped response reaching the judge fails *open* rather than reading as a verdict, and — by AST parse, in `test_core_boundary.py`'s style — that the module reaches no database and no provider directly. | 🟡 |

### S4c off-site destination options

Set in `.env` (directory must already exist and be writable — the script never creates it):

```
BACKUP_OFFSITE_DIR=/absolute/path/to/existing/folder
```

1. **Cloud-synced folder** (zero-credential on this Mac) — iCloud Drive / Dropbox / Google Drive desktop. Script writes a file; the sync client moves it off-machine.
   - **iCloud:** turn off "Optimise Mac Storage" for that folder/machine, or expect `.english_bot_*.dump.icloud` placeholders. Freshness and retention treat placeholders as present (healthy). Dropbox / Google Drive have equivalent selective-sync / online-only behaviour.
2. **`rsync`** to a second host (Hetzner-era): e.g. `rsync -av ~/english-bot-backups/ user@offsite:/path/`.
3. **`rclone`** to object storage (Hetzner-era): e.g. `rclone copy ~/english-bot-backups remote:bucket/`.

No cloud SDK or credentials live in this repo.

### S15a watched-folder layout

Set in `.env` (directory must already exist outside the repo — bot never invents a Drive mount):

```
WATCH_DIR=/absolute/path/to/existing/folder
```

Auto-created under `WATCH_DIR` for each registered user:

```
inbox/<telegram_user_id>/
  trancy/
  language_reactor/
outbox/<telegram_user_id>/
processed/<telegram_user_id>/
failed/<telegram_user_id>/
```

On this Mac: point at a Google Drive–synced folder (no credentials). **On Hetzner prod: `WATCH_DIR` deliberately unset** — no Drive client; S15a dormant; CSV via Telegram (S15b) only. `rclone` + Google service account remains an open option if the folder bridge is ever needed on the server.

**Operator logs (S18 / prod):** `journalctl -u english-bot -f` (preferred on Hetzner); also `tail -f /home/bot/english-bot-runtime/bot.log`. Message content never appears in either.

## Verification checklist

Do not start S9b until these are cleared or explicitly deferred.

### How to verify

1. Start the bot: `.venv/bin/python -m app.main`
2. Watch the terminal alongside Telegram.
3. Tail the rotating log: `tail -f ~/english-bot-runtime/bot.log`

- [ ] **S8** — second correct after a winner → “already claimed”, no second point

## Next action

**W5b is an investigation. It fixes nothing, and that is the deliverable.** No
migration, no API change, no frontend change, no server step, no `packages/core`
change to any existing file. **`schema_version` stays at 12**; 013 belongs to W7.
Suite **1340 → 1366 passing / 6 skipped / 0 failing**; **Vitest 52**, unchanged.
`git diff --stat` is **empty** — the slice is two new files.

### What the tree already answered, before a call was billed

**#115 was a false dichotomy and both of its branches are wrong.** The gate hands
`judge_naturalness` the string `checks.sentence_of(item)` returns, which for
`mcq`, `cloze_cued` and `collocation_pick` is the **gapped stem** and for
`error_spot` is **the sentence with its deliberate error still in it**. The
prompt asks *"would a real person say this to a friend?"*, never mentions gaps or
exercises, and instructs the model to reject anything *"nobody actually phrases
that way"*. **A model that rejects `"I ___ to the shops yesterday."` is obeying
that instruction correctly.**

The dry run prints the partition and it is perfect, eleven of eleven:

```
mcq                    A: 'I ___ to the shops yesterday.'  <-- GAPPED/ERRORED
cloze_cued             A: 'I ___ to the shops yesterday.'  <-- GAPPED/ERRORED
error_spot             A: 'I goed to the shops'            <-- GAPPED/ERRORED
collocation_pick       A: 'She ___ a photo of us at the beach.'  <-- GAPPED/ERRORED
word_bank_order        A: 'I went to the shops'
l1_to_l2_production    A: 'I went to the shop yesterday'
dictation              A: 'I forgot my keys and had to wait outside'
listening_gap          A: "She doesn't like coffee these days"
speak_repeat           A: "I'll call you back in a minute"
speak_answer           A: 'What did you do last weekend?'
match_pairs            (not judged — sentence_of returns "")

10 judged, 4 of them gapped or errored, 6 already prose.
```

Every `unnatural` verdict is a defective judged string. Every clean-prose string
passed. The fifth rejection, `listening_gap`, passed naturalness and failed at
the **probe** — a different gate, filed as **#121**.

**There is no threshold**, so the fix everyone reaches for first does not exist:
the verdict is a raw boolean and a missing row fails *open*.

### What is still unknown, and it is one question

**Does the judge accept the same sentence with the gap closed?** That is the
counterfactual, it is not derivable from code, and it is **arm B**. Until it is
run the finding is *cause identified*, not *cause proven* — **#115 is updated and
deliberately not closed.**

### Run W5b — on the Mac. It does not touch production.

This opens no database connection, writes nothing, and never contacts the shared
host. It needs only `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in the repo-root `.env`. **No backup, no
migration, no restart, no deploy.**

**1. Dry run. Sends nothing.**

```
python -m core.items.judge_observe
```

*Evidence:* the model name, the naturalness prompt in full, and both arms for
every fixture. Ends `Nothing was sent and nothing was written.` The four
`GAPPED/ERRORED` lines above should be visible. **This is most of the finding at
zero cost.**

**2. The live observation. 76 calls on `claude-sonnet-5`, under $0.30.**

```
python -m core.items.judge_observe --live 2>&1 | tee /tmp/w5b-judge.txt
```

Prints the call count and waits for a typed `y` before sending. *Evidence:* every
run's verdict **and the model's one-word reason**, a per-fixture summary, arm C's
probe sets, the four pre-registered predictions marked HELD or FAILED, and the
verdict the branch rules give. **Paste the SUMMARY, PREDICTIONS and VERDICT
blocks back.** The branch rules are in the module docstring and are not to be
adjusted to the result.

**3. The suite.**

```
pytest -q
```

*Evidence:* **1366 passed, 6 skipped, 0 failed.**

**4. Confirm the slice changed nothing it was told not to change.**

```
git status --short && git diff --stat
```

*Evidence:* two new untracked files and an **empty** diff. Any modification to
`gates.py`, `checks.py`, `naturalness.py`, `packages/core/prompts/` or
`valid.json` means the slice did the thing it was barred from doing — stop.

**5. Optional, production, only as belt and braces for #109.** W5b never contacts
this host, so this re-confirms W6a rather than checking W5b. The
`SELECT count(*) FROM items` command is the last deployment step already written
below. *Evidence:* still **0**.

### What the fix slice has to rule on — not decided here

1. **Give the judge prose.** A `judged_sentence()` that fills the gap and
   corrects the error tile; `sentence_of` keeps its job for the mechanical rules.
   **Cost if declined:** four item types that can never be generated (#115).
2. **Stop discarding the `reason`** (#119). This slice exists because one string
   was dropped one line before anything could read it.
3. **Add the offline input invariant** — `GAP not in judged_sentence(item)` over
   `valid.json`. It is one line, needs no model, and **fails today on four
   fixtures**, which is why W5b names it instead of writing it: a red test would
   break this slice's own bar and an `xfail` would be the quiet bar-lowering
   CLAUDE.md §3 rule 7 forbids.
4. **`listening_gap` needs its own decision** (#121). If the probe is blind to
   the audio, the gate's design changes for that type — not the fixture.
5. **`JUDGE_BATCH`** (#120) is a W10 budget line, not tidiness.

### Still unrun, carried forward — every one, named and not silently

**Checks 1 and 2 are W6's own acceptance criteria and are still unrun.** Neither
is cleared by W5b, which touched no frontend.

1. **A typed answer with the correct words, wrong capitalisation and a trailing
   full stop, on the phone's own keyboard with autocorrect live.** **W6
   acceptance criterion 2, still unrun** — the 2026-08-25 attempt typed a
   genuinely wrong answer, so it proved the wrong path worked and said nothing
   about folding. `autoCorrect="off"` is a *request* to iOS Safari; whether it is
   honoured is an empirical question about a device, and jsdom has no autocorrect.

5. **The 20-item Life sample.** W5's own check; it needs *generated* items, so it

6. **`/stats` in Telegram on the restructured bot.** W1's, and the last of that
   pair.
7. **The four Telegram explanation paths — `/diary`, a voice message, `/capture`,
   a `/talk` close-out. Deprioritised 2026-08-24**, not declined.
8. **The S25 pre-flight counts from production**, for the decisions log.
9. **#44 — evening reading**, until a real Mon/Wed/Fri delivery lands.
10. **S8** — the shared group, `/here`, `COUPLE_CHAT_ID`, then the seven checks.
11. **The entire v2 desk-check list, unchanged.**

### What genuinely remains, in order

2. **#121 — the blind solver is blind to the audio.** New, `high`, and it may

**Also open and unchanged:** **#83** (two hosts can poll one bot token — high,

**#84**, **#85**, **#86**, **#100** (three stale Build columns in
